**Purpose.** Keep missing contracts, source records, institutional evidence, appointments, and consequential architecture choices visible for later intellectual work. This queue is not a plan/register edit, is not a gate, and does not suspend engineering whose behavior can be implemented and verified without those premises. Each item names the claim it limits and a concrete closure signal. An unresolved premise must travel as typed `not_established`/`UNRUN`; it must not be replaced by a guessed contract.

**Snapshot and denominator.** The census snapshot is clean `codex/e02-r2` at `2cd18f45a0e0ee34ee7b36e1eb332124d4540d20` (tree `9eb595fd04f9fa64432e97b7c8415f076ce7c3ea`); its code/test evidence head is `d103d234cc114ef9a5b725de1d9a460b61ebeea0`. The census script parses the complete ledger from that pinned snapshot: **282 rows, 282 unique IDs (225 B + 57 LA)**. It selects the union of all 12 `held`, the single `open`, and every row whose typed owner is `owner_decision`, `principal_decision`, `owner_appointment`, `data_record`, `external_institution`, or `smallest_missing_capability`: **56 rows (12 held, 1 open, 43 partial)**. Kind counts are 9 data records, 15 external-institution dependencies, 8 owner appointments, 6 owner decisions, 13 principal decisions, 4 smallest missing capabilities, and B09's 1 open implementation-owner case. The census script verifies exact coverage of the selected union by the curated blocker catalog: 56/56 IDs, no missing or extra IDs, and supplies a source-card locator for each of the 56 selected rows. Where the ledger has no explicit source-card URI (12 selected rows), it derives the locator only from that row's `source_ref` and `source_line` against the exact source-card blob and checks the line is the matching finding heading; `source_card_ref_origin` distinguishes those derived navigators from recorded card refs. The complete per-row residuals, owner states, triage locators, behavioral-verdict fields, and source-card locators are in the census output linked below; the IDs in this document are links into that source of truth, not duplicate finding records.

**Delivery snapshot.** Reviewed commit `6a607b0b2` followed the census and changed only `BASELINES.md`, `DECISION_RECORDS.md`, `FINAL_REPORT.md`, and `REGISTER_PROPOSALS.md`. The 282-row ledger is unchanged. The counts and citations here remain pinned to `2cd18f45a`; later documentation is not silently substituted as census input.

## Near-term frontier: record the limit, keep building

### OP-R1-TIME — source-time contract for the five-input DataState WMR

- **Kind / IDs:** source contract plus source evidence; B01–B03 (all remain partial).
- **Question / missing evidence:** what source-authoritative temporal meaning, granularity, interval-boundary and selection/aggregation rule applies to each of DataState's five actual input files? The inspected source metadata/fixtures do not establish a common contract. Current projection declares `2021-12/2023-07`, while its calculations read latest/full-file or arbitrary capped populations; declared WMR scope is not proof of the rows consumed. This is **verified missing only in the complete five-input metadata/fixture inspection scope**, not a claim that no authoritative documentation exists elsewhere. R1 memo: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r1-datastate-provenance-20260928/R1_DATASTATE_SOURCE_TIME_PROVENANCE.md@sha256:9b9c1cd2b9cd1144c09408bf3f03e63ba5ae5763728307f686d2cf37c76f511a`; the later owner decision gives the implementation-level interval details in `DECISION_RECORDS.md` §R1 S1.
- **Claim affected:** whether this WMR is in scope for a particular N5 candidate calculation; a temporal label alone cannot support current-scope or S8 authority.
- **Owner state:** existing DataState/S1 projection owner is identified. It may implement only source-supported semantics. The source-time evidence remains `not_established`; no new source signer/appointment is inferred.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** record source meaning and permitted selection for all five inputs, derive coverage from the selected bytes/rows, bind the record to the WMR, then show in-range expected values, evidenced out-of-range behavior, unknown-time handling, and a marker-retaining interval mutation that turns the test red. If changing only out-of-range rows leaves the admitted projected values unchanged, or an unsupported period reaches a current-scope claim, the proposed rule is falsified.
- **Safe engineering now:** build the controlled-profile owner path and synthetic-Parquet behavioral tests; ordinary N4 remains available. Use an evidence-supported historical profile only. If the N5 result cannot carry unknown temporal coverage, mark this WMR-backed N5 disposition `not_established`/not run. Do not compare date strings or infer time from the Fabric query timestamp.

### OP-R1-SYMBOLIC-MATERIALIZATION — N4 do-assignment has no demonstrated numeric N5 materializer (2026-09-30)

- **Relation / typed gap:** candidate-band producer/owner-contract premise within B01–B03 and the existing R1 N4→N5 route; no new finding row, ledger status, or denominator change. The smallest missing capability is an owner-produced mapping from a grounded operator/parameter to concrete direct assignments.
- **Observed boundary (P37/P38):** N4 emits `VariableAssignment(value=None, value_expr=...)` containing a descriptive `do(<slot>) via <kind> params_ref=<digest>` expression. Binding preserves this expression; the variable mapper does not evaluate it. Numeric NCM/coupled/system-dynamics consumers require a concrete value. ProgramGraph has a separate parameter-override input, but the inspected path does not derive overrides from the grounded atom. N5 completion or candidate identity therefore does not prove that the intervention changed an engine input/output.
- **Owner / missing contract:** typed state is `producer_missing` for concrete materialization in the inspected N4→N5 path. Extend the existing L6 `resolve_intervention_lever()` / atom-binding owners, not a second expression evaluator: they expose parameter domain and target slots, but no inspected producer converts the grounded atom/expression into numeric direct assignments. Assignment semantics, units, and replayable materialization are `not_established`; the supported rule must bind operator/parameter identity, numeric type and units, domain, target slot, and set-to/offset semantics. Retain a typed limitation until this owner path supplies it.
- **Claim affected / safe work:** whether N5 computes `P(Y | do(X=x))` rather than an ordinary engine run with an attached N4 identity. Ordinary candidate production and controlled N4→N5 requests remain available; do not claim a numerical intervention effect or promote it. A consumer that cannot use the expression must return a typed simulation limitation. This premise does not decide S8, N9, or publication.
- **Closure signal / falsifier:** through the existing owner, materialize a source-bound concrete value with replayable provenance; with other inputs fixed, assert the exact N5 intervention input and specified downstream output. Remove only materialization while retaining candidate/source/receipt markers; the positive must turn red. Keep a no-owner ordinary-candidate control and reject unsupported operator, parameter, unit, or slot semantics. A test-only scalar substituted for `value_expr` is not closure.
- **Evidence:** design trace `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r1-symbolic-materialization-20260930/R1_N4_N5_SYMBOLIC_MATERIALIZATION_DESIGN.md@sha256:c570abeb1fff0993f9873963c290fe671db3a2e71b1fab9d93b7266a872a7f2f`; source snapshots `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/design_generation.py@sha256:e754139eb098db5e4ae393abf4f583e22b9149778a756d83cf32cb47ea7612fd`, `intervention_substrate.py@sha256:1783a4ecfd67e0010e4e4ccc5c19a022da5786e517c70f356cd5f639cbc726df`, `intervention_atom_binding.py@sha256:50edc3d56f756f099cb42de02361a2cee09426e4996d961dedbc2b4534847153`, `generation_cycle.py@sha256:8fdb8e11e5973746f06e6e31cc42fad889b849b1b4dd703432de6df84b4f82a4`, and `joint_simulation_horizon.py@sha256:3721594ea3d2828e32f4acaf33676348e61d30c330d2f62f78d37ebb2f2f8ef6`. These are source/design evidence, not test receipts.

### OP-R1-CREDAL-VINTAGE — restricted L2 confidence blocks the recorded candidate reference

- **Kind / scope:** byte-bound source-vintage data restriction plus a candidate-path behavior gap; B01–B03 remain partial. This is a premise addendum, not a new finding row; the 282-row denominator is unchanged.

#### OP-R13-ACQ candidate-slot divergence (2026-09-30)

The W1 candidate at `8ae5f9583` adds a `materialize_acquisition_candidate_data_snapshot` helper. Foundry's `build_input_bindings` reads that `data_ref`, not the evidence ref. A new snapshot ID and matching WMR/SKG ID strings therefore cannot prove that N5 simulated the acquired row. The candidate helper has no production caller at this pin. This is a measured example of the existing B12/R13 `bridge_missing` class, not an additional finding or a positive world-growth witness. Until that limitation propagates, a served success cannot claim the selected row was simulated, advanced the world, or justified S8/promotion. A new field in a hashed receipt requires a new version and historical serializer; do not reinterpret v2 bytes.

#### OP-R2-N6 clarification — temporal replay of a currentness disposition (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / status:** subordinate to OP-R2-N6 and its served N6-currentness clarification; same R2/P40 class, no new B/LA row, status change, denominator, register proposal, or plan task. This records a temporal replay escape in the proposed typed-blocked consumer slice; it does not revise the package/deployment issuer premise or assert an external issuer is absent.
- **Observed owner/projection mismatch:** `NormativeValueScheduleOwner._generation_disposition` calls strict `validate_generation_cycle_run`, which composes currentness from the Confidence Ledger’s live `N6DeploymentCurrentnessObservation`. `project_generation_disposition` then recomputes `_generation_disposition` at the stored `admitted_at` and requires equality with the stored disposition. If the owner observation changes after a blocked disposition is written (for example, its census readiness or the loaded deployment identity changes), identical persisted N6 bytes can yield a different currentness issue/reason tuple. Historical disposition replay then fails as `p20_normative_generation_disposition_mismatch`; the implementation has no separate historical owner-observation projection or typed currentness transition in that path.
- **Schema and typed blocker:** `NormativeGenerationDisposition.v1` can encode a current-call blocked/no-ranking result using its existing status, empty rankings, and reason-code fields. That shape alone is insufficient to preserve a replayable record of the owner observation that caused the block: the v1 disposition stores no N6 observation/ref, and the existing `project_generation_disposition` recomputes against current state. The smallest missing capability is `artifact_missing` for a source-bound historical currentness observation and `bridge_missing` for replaying that immutable record separately from a fresh authority-currentness query. This may use a versioned persisted observation or an explicit versioned historical projection plus a separate current projection, through the existing S8/ConfidenceLedger owners. Do not infer or manufacture a deployment issuer, census PASS, external authority, or reissue path from this finding.
- **Safe independent engineering:** a current invocation may still use the existing disposition shape to return blocked/no-ranking when exact history/intrinsic checks pass and the complete strict issue tuple is the singleton canonical projection of the typed owner observation. Preserve exact reason/census/unresolved fields, make clear S8 evidence was not evaluated, and do not read S8 permission or call `recommend` on that path. Keep source-custody, intrinsic/history, terminal-block, and mixed issues separate. Ordinary candidate computation, exact N6 historical-byte verification, and source-free reader work remain useful and independent. Do not describe that current-view slice as closing persisted temporal replay.
- **Transition falsifier / acceptance:** create a disposition for an exact content-addressed N6 source while the owner observation is `not_established`/`UNRUN`; retain the stored disposition bytes/ref and N6 CAS bytes. Change only the live owner observation for the same N6 bytes to `current` or `stale`. Historical replay must still validate and return the original blocked bytes/reason against the versioned historical N6 serializer; a fresh current projection must separately return its exact typed observation and remain blocked/reissue-required, never authorize from the old disposition. It must not collapse into generic disposition mismatch. Assert no S8 recommendation/authority was produced from the old artifact. A marker-retaining removal of the historical/current split or observation binding must make this transition assertion red. An issue-only removal that leaves the canonical owner observation must fail closed on the issue/observation mismatch; that is a discriminator-integrity test, not the currentness-property removal probe. A matching-currentness S8-positive or full owner-adjudication removal probe remains separately `UNRUN` until an eligible source exists.
- **R1 and served boundary:** the reviewed R1 owner-bound `simulate_only` N4→N5 witness ends with S8 `not_run`; it does not supply the served S8/currentness transition source. Keep the proposed `_process_control_job` S8 witness `UNRUN` until an eligible protected/EvalSafety source or existing owner-bound completed Core source is demonstrated. This does not block owner-level candidate engineering.
- **P37/P38/P40:** currentness status is supplied by the Confidence Ledger owner. In the reviewed repository scope the package/deployment issuer remains `producer_missing`; external issuer existence remains `UNRUN`, and census readiness remains `UNRUN`. The disposition consumer re-evaluates live currentness while purporting to replay a stored result. The divergent case is unchanged N6 bytes with changed live owner currentness: the present consumer returns generic disposition mismatch instead of historical replay plus a separate current typed result. This is the same R2 currentness/history consumer class one level deeper, not a new class; widen the shared owner/projection mechanism once rather than add per-schema or per-reason exceptions.
- **Evidence and source pins:** independent design challenge `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r2-currentness-consumer-design-review-20260929.md@sha256:f7c4605d909e3cbf999e85daeda5fd2fc2a0b3c24243722301e7d994ebc63dcd`; at integration head `a3b432027c43c5af652415273bcbad01b83f8269`, relevant blobs are `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/design_axes/value_choice_provenance.py@git-blob:f42f5b2b4be92fe723c488985c9861ef3da17aa6`, `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/generation_cycle.py@git-blob:d85f891e50fd66b25b1857aca95631912a84a901`, `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/confidence_ledger.py@git-blob:0cfa16945529172584b88af2d5db0e53d4e226ab`, and `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/services/control/run_lifecycle.py@git-blob:44408ec780524453e39650070031f0c8a6875bc4`. Relevant existing controls are `test_historical_v1_identity_and_custody_limits_block_s8_with_exact_replay`, `test_missing_deployment_identity_does_not_refuse_candidate_computation`, and the served R1 `simulate_only` control in `test_normative_generation_bridge.py`; this design addendum claims no new test result.

### OP-R4-N11-LIFETIME — risk-budget scope across a revised basis

- **Kind:** owner/principal architecture decision; supplemental to the 282 B/LA rows and not a new finding row.
- **Question / missing evidence:** should N11's non-resettable risk-spend budget follow stable subject `S = design_problem_ref` through revised bases `B = design_problem_basis_ref`, or should each exact basis get a distinct budget? Current `ConfidenceRiskBudgetScope.scope_id` does not distinguish full projection hashes, while the ledger root binds the complete scope; re-opening the same subject ID with changed `DesignProblem` therefore yields `ledger_scope_binding_mismatch`. Code establishes current refusal mechanics, not the policy for carrying/resetting budget across basis revisions.
- **Claim affected:** whether cumulative N11 risk spend remains conserved for one continuing policy subject across N6 revisions, or is independently budgeted for each exact problem basis.
- **Owner state:** Confidence Ledger / N11 scope owner is identified; no owner/principal choice for cross-basis lifetime is recorded. The existing source behavior fails closed.
- **Options / cost:** (1) one subject-wide non-resettable budget, with per-basis N9 projection and an authorized typed basis-transition record; cost is schema/projection and historical replay work, benefit is no budget reset through revision. (2) one budget per exact basis; cost is an explicit anti-reset/non-transfer rule because a changed basis would otherwise acquire fresh spend, benefit is exact-snapshot scope. (3) keep current refusal pending an authorized transition; N9 remains unavailable across changed-basis reuse while candidate work continues.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** decide which identity owns the budget; exercise a served second run with same `design_problem_id` and a changed full basis; demonstrate the chosen carry/reset behavior through the N11 owner and preserve history only through its named transition. Keep a marker-retaining removal probe that strips the selected basis/transition binding and must turn the positive red. Any path that silently reuses or resets a ledger against a changed full scope falsifies the policy.
- **Safe engineering now:** retain the current `ledger_scope_binding_mismatch` refusal; continue N4/N5 candidate work, source-free N6 verifier/issuer handoff work, and R4 session-factory tests in a controlled owner-approved lane. Do not change ledger keys, delete/reissue roots, or reuse a durable N11 ledger under a new full-problem hash.
- **Evidence:** `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r4-root-cause-audit-20260928/R4_R14_ROOT_CAUSE_AUDIT.md@sha256:d8e24c5341fdea0f3e2236d20e4e4d3a095e18a9cc2b5fb04313a54f013becb9`; relevant owner source references in that audit: `promotion_sequence.py:3491-3505`, `confidence_ledger.py:1856-1885,204-228,2749-2773,5973-5985@a477fd88f`.

The exact-head architecture guardrail was not rerun at patch-base source/test HEAD `8177440fdc3b86bffa479b7b8bfe1729bf968484` (`UNRUN`); do not transfer the 3ec verdict to that newer code. Diagnosis `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r2-openapi-eb1733750-20260928/OPENAPI_OWNER_DIAGNOSIS.md@sha256:a01f32e0d0000400d5539a929ba1451e5f0a4f5494c56880f5f8e50f69b14bf1` and complete leaf delta `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r2-openapi-eb1733750-20260928/openapi-owner-delta.json@sha256:950790ca6c9d5e8f6f2477c4153ffe8a5ec3f28ba21f247376e3bfc06ad9e228` establish repeatable bytes at eb and a measured snapshot-value mismatch. ## Evidence state and use rules

1. `held`, `open`, or a typed blocker is a classification from the ledger, not proof that all possible sources were searched. The census stores the row's `finding_level_verdict`, card discriminator, and cited source-card ref; the common `record_inspection_probe` with `UNRUN` does **not** mean behavior failed.
2. Call absence **verified missing** only within the exact complete census/owner boundary cited by that premise (e.g. 5,906 tracked Python source/tool/test/example paths; 2,696 production-source callers; 126 Phase-0 inventory paths). Outside that boundary, keep `UNRUN` or `not_established`. A grep hit count is never a set-completeness claim.
3. The crosswalk's relation is scoped: a direct touch is not advancement or closure; the full matrix is cited from the source document. Do not convert shared vocabulary into a new GY/Atlas/register edge.
4. For every new premise, preserve: stable ID; typed kind; binding claim; named owner plus whether an appointment exists; falsifier/closure signal; what engineering can proceed without it; source path@sha. Update only this queue and the proper evidence package; Denis rules on principal choices, the architect lands register changes, and authorized owners perform artifact reissue.
5. This list does not block independent work: source-independent tests, mechanism design, owner wiring, synthetic adversarial probes, typed candidate limitations, and non-authoritative demonstrations may proceed where they do not make the unresolved authority claim.

### OP-R9-CACHE-CUSTODY — guarded proxy and cloud exact-view/signature boundary

- **Kind / IDs:** R9/CAS-01 follow-up: one runtime-proxy verification gap and one cloud signed-evidence contract/owner boundary. This is a premise addendum, not a new ledger row; B150 remains closed only for its direct `FileSystemCAS` view property, while the broader R9 class stays partial.
- **Measured slice:** integrated commit `68caebd84a5102a92eb93453c5a1bf0ca9ea2455` changes the composite cache read to resolve the durable writer's manifest before admitting a local exact-profile view. The focused cache JUnit is 27/27, zero failures/errors/skips (`/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r9-integrated-20260929/caching-store-68ca.junit.xml@sha256:15018cac37f4138d2571ce1a6e6a43e6035cd4d54af5fdc231f05df24f6c03bd`). A separate S3/GCS backend JUnit is 11/11, zero failures/errors/skips; it covers fake-client manifest-view, integrity and namespace cases only, not signature custody or deployed IAM (`/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r9-integrated-20260929/cloud-backends-9a4a.junit.xml@sha256:13b22cb78ee734a20bc6fdce847494febab4695d52eb37196d2a675031d7a9ed`). Independent review is GO for the frozen cache slice and explicitly bounds the residuals (`/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r9-readonly-design-20260929/R9_FROZEN_CANDIDATE_REVIEW.md@sha256:c79a22b834e73880036695f92f25f01420b7e44671b169229a8117a967cc2e0b`). These receipts do not close the broader class.
- **Claim affected:** the bounded cache behavior supports selecting the durable owner's profile when resolving a content ID. It does not establish an authority-grade tenant denial or signed-evidence claim across the served guarded-store and S3/GCS paths.
- **Verified evidence gap — runtime proxy:** the existing tenant-child case and R9 suite use `FileSystemCAS` directly. No cited witness exercises a denied read through the runtime-bound `GuardedDependencyProxy`. Source inspection says a selector-free read asks the durable owner's manifest first and propagates remote `PermissionError`; the execution through the proxy is `not_established`. A local `has_manifest_view` ownership denial becomes a cache miss, so the implementation may fall through to the durable owner. Whether a local denial must abort even when that owner authorizes is a separate policy question, not established by these receipts.
- **Contract boundary — S3/GCS signed custody:** both cloud backend owners and multi-profile manifest-view code/tests exist. The 11/11 fake-client run checks integrity and parsed profile selection, not byte-exact manifest/signature custody through the composite path or deployment IAM behavior. More specifically, the generic `ArtifactStore` contract excludes signing, and the current `FileSystemSignedArtifactEvidenceRepository` requires the exact-byte filesystem-CAS surface. There is no admitted S3/GCS signed-evidence adapter/owner in these sources. Thus signature custody for these backends is not merely an unrun test: its backend-neutral contract/bridge is `absent/unallocated`; do not infer cloud signature support from manifest-view support.
- **Owner/appointment state:** in-tree owners exist for `ArtifactStore`, `CachingArtifactStore`, the runtime guard proxy, S3/GCS manifest views, and filesystem signed-evidence handling. The particular deployed cloud principal/bucket policy and an owner appointment for a cloud signed-evidence adapter are not evidenced by these receipts. Do not label the whole artifact-store owner absent or guess a cloud custody policy.
- **P37 / P38:** the durable manifest's selected profile and raw cache-import integrity are `recomputed` in the bounded local tests; current remote-owner tenant authorization through the actual proxy and cloud IAM are `not_established` here (IAM is `institutionally_supplied` only once tied to a named configured principal). The tested predicate is local profile selection and cache bytes. P38 divergence: a green direct-`FileSystemCAS` suite is a proxy for the wider guarded/deployed claim; the distinguishing case is a tenant denial crossing the executor-backed proxy while the cache holds a matching blob, or a selected cloud profile whose detached signature is absent, stale, or bound to another raw manifest. Neither case is decided by the 27/27 cache result or the 11/11 backend result.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** (1) run a served-path negative with the actual guard proxy around tenant-bound stores: a durable-owner denial must reach the caller without returning locally cached bytes; retain an authorized same-tenant exact-view positive control. Add a separate owner disposition for local-cache denial with remote authorization and test the selected rule. (2) For S3 and GCS, assert exact selected raw manifest bytes across cache population and profile/default movement. Signed use additionally needs an explicit owner/contract decision: either route through an admitted backend-neutral exact-byte evidence owner and prove the signature binds the selected raw manifest and blob for each supported backend, or mark that authority surface out of scope and refuse the signed claim there. Falsifier: a denied remote read returns cached bytes, a selector change alters selected raw bytes without refusal, or verification accepts a sidecar bound to another manifest.
- **Safe engineering now:** strengthen fake-client S3/GCS raw-view tests and add the guarded-proxy denial/authorized control through existing owners. Keep these as candidate storage behavior; no signed authority follows. Map any signature consumer to the current exact-byte owner and record the backend gap; do not add a second signing owner or claim real-cloud IAM without a named deployment witness.
- **Sources:** `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/backends/caching_store.py@git-blob:58004a153f3bc00460d0ed118c38b27bb5168ec2`; `policy-engine/tests/unit/core/artifacts/backends/test_caching_store.py@git-blob:aa60e2554de4eabd49926400337123efc5b5e07c`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/protocol.py@git-blob:cbb89b172c4dadd6d84bb570827e86664c242eee`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/signed_evidence.py@git-blob:c95349fa5f1650d2f68ceee6ffe8d9acc1472a83`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/resilience.py@git-blob:3e3fce3e300fa84d3f63f73643a94dfb3b1d4d3b`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/backends/s3_store.py@git-blob:556563755eb2bb4a147837a0919fe02cb699babb`; `policy-engine/tests/unit/core/artifacts/backends/test_s3_store.py@git-blob:997c8d612bb546d4463df839e4c984734de371c8`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/backends/gcs_store.py@git-blob:10f8ac81bc043cd3cf3cc5cc023b51f7c9803062`; `policy-engine/tests/unit/core/artifacts/backends/test_gcs_store.py@git-blob:5577b9fa37db78580918bfa1c420b670cb3ac466`; bounded B150 record: `policy-engine/docs/plans/active/agent-packages/PolicyOS_E02R2/RESIDUAL_LEDGER.md@git-blob:21ff9df91ff78be98dc03118a7d98c9ee86448e5#B150`.

### OP-R11-G4-REPLAY-CONTEXT — deployment-bound G4 record for served N9 replay

- **Kind / IDs:** supplemental smallest-missing-capability premise for the N9 receipt-replay bridge; related to R11 but not a new B/LA finding row. The ledger remains 282/282 and the selected blocker census remains 56/282. This note does not change any finding status.
- **Question / missing evidence:** how does the served EvalSafety/N9 receipt replay resolve the exact G4 governed-promotion record for the canonical loaded deployment without depending on a source checkout or process working directory? At b8, CanonicalPromotionInput supplies a default G4 record ref; the N9 validator resolves it through _resolve_g4_governed_promotion_record, which reads (repo_root or Path.cwd()) / architecture/policy_design_case/layer3_g4_promotion_records.json. The served EvaluationSafetyPromotionSourceContext carries neither repo_root nor a typed G4 record resolver, and verify_near_miss_classification calls canonical receipt validation without repo_root. A complete name/path census over 2,696 tracked src/polisyos Python files found the N9-specific file resolver in promotion_sequence.py; G4 record models/builders and other G4 artifact loaders exist, but this serving path has no deployment-bound resolver/context bridge.
- **Distinct from the v6 fixture error:** v6's synthetic receipt was produced with repo_root=REPO_ROOT and replayed without it. That mismatch explains the earlier G4 obligation/hash red; the current persisted-source test now supplies the same root to its producer and replay precondition. It does not resolve the serving context gap. The current b8 selector/full-file replay is still UNRUN in the cited audit.
- **Claim affected:** whether a valid, nonblocked N9 receipt can be revalidated for served EvalSafety near-miss classification when the runtime is packaged or started outside the source-tree root. This does not weaken R11's blocked-N6 exclusion and does not make G4 record production itself absent.
- **Owner state:** G4 record types and source/artifact producers exist. The missing capability is the typed N9 replay resolver/context bound to the canonical deployment or runtime-owned artifact source. A deployment/package owner appointment and the actual installed G4 record source are not_established; do not infer either from the repository working tree. Packaging configuration omits this JSON from its explicit sdist include list and declares only code packages plus one unrelated forced resource for the wheel, but no built install artifact or deployment manifest was inspected.
- **P37 / P38:** parsing the selected local JSON and matching the declared record ID are recomputed. The decisive premise that these bytes are the record authorized for the loaded deployment is not_established at the serving verifier. P38 divergence: the same receipt can be admitted when CWD happens to contain the source-root architecture tree and refused when CWD is unrelated or the runtime has no checkout, because CWD currently selects the G4 record source.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** produce and validate one genuine N9 receipt through its canonical owner, then exercise the actual served EvalSafety classifier from an unrelated empty CWD with the source checkout unavailable and only the declared deployment/runtime artifact owners. The resolver must select the same exact G4 record and replay the receipt; absence or a mismatched deployment binding must yield a typed limitation/UNRUN or refusal. Remove the resolver/deployment binding while retaining the G4 reference and record markers; the semantic test must turn red. Inspect the built install artifact and name its owner before claiming the JSON is or is not shipped.
- **Safe engineering now:** preserve the blocked-N6 status guard and same-root fixture setup; continue unrelated candidate-band work. Do not weaken receipt validation, silently infer a source root, or make this premise a blocker for R11 code whose behavior does not depend on G4 replay.

### OP-R13-S8-STORE-GUARD — production normative owner bypasses the proxy guard

- **Relation / status:** supplemental R13/P31 same-class residual; no new B/LA row, finding-status change, or change to the 282-row denominator.
- **Affected claim and measured divergence:** the production S8 normative-value path receives the runtime-supplied guarded CAS, but its normative owner adapter unwraps GuardedDependencyProxy._target to satisfy a concrete FileSystemCAS requirement. build_runtime_api_context wraps the ambient-ownership-enforced store with guard_runtime_cas; RunLifecycle passes that store to normative_owner_for_runtime_store; the adapter selects _target; NormativeValueScheduleOwner then calls the target directly. This skips the proxy's guarded executor path, including its timeout/circuit-breaker behavior. It does **not** by itself prove a tenant-authorization leak: the target was already ambient-ownership-enforced, and this review did not demonstrate cross-tenant access.
- **Owner / smallest missing capability:** Runtime Control and the GenerationCycle normative owner own this consumer boundary; Core ArtifactStore and runtime resilience own the guarded-store contract. What is absent is a typed normative-store interface or adapter that preserves the supplied guard for every signed-artifact read and write while satisfying the owner's operations, or an owner-approved equivalent that proves the guard remains active. The concrete-type seam is where implementation diverges from the intended property.
- **Falsifier / closure signal:** exercise served S8 resolution with the production supplied store and instrument guard execution for each persisted CAS operation. Keep request, artifact, and scope markers while replacing guarded calls with direct _target calls; the positive guard witness must turn red. Separately retain a foreign-tenant negative and an authorized same-tenant positive; only an observed unauthorized read would establish a tenant leak. Reopen this residual if the direct-call mutant stays green or if production resolution does not pass through this adapter.
- **Safe engineering now:** source-free R2 historical replay/currentness work, ordinary candidate-band N4/N5 work, and the independent R13 selected-overlay integrity and scale measurement can continue. Keep S8 authority claims bounded until the served guard witness exists; no CAS redesign or tenant-leak claim follows from unwrapping alone.
- **Evidence:** policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/dependencies.py@git-blob:6ef6f03333aae79bdf48f2e9fefbec7737d960f0; policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/services/control/run_lifecycle.py@git-blob:be50a2752d210d299a4a9c92ca424f5888da8b28; policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/services/control/generation_cycle.py@git-blob:471a80e2db0d93cec5a4c19d5970c1ddb9182e63; policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/design_axes/value_choice_provenance.py@git-blob:11c08cdb2ad314e836dd43f2f4b86ee1e2d10d3d; policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/http/resilience.py@git-blob:3e3fce3e300fa84d3f63f73643a94dfb3b1d4d3b; adapter test policy-engine/tests/unit/runtime/http/test_normative_generation_bridge.py@git-blob:ef8ec18a438afe790a0b03f35f90b9e90d6288d4. ### OP-R13-P31-CONFIDENCE-LEDGER — N9 CAS writer, replay reader, and journal scope (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / status:** supplemental R13/P31 same-class premise, distinct from `OP-R13-S8-STORE-GUARD`; no new B/LA row, finding-status change, denominator change, or authority claim. The read-only census found a production N9 confidence-ledger store path that is not covered by the S8 normative-owner adapter finding.
- **Affected claim / writer seam (`bridge_missing`):** `CanonicalN9PromotionPort._open_confidence_ledger_session()` calls `ConfidenceLedgerSession.from_repo()`. That opener checks the repo root against the loaded deployment root and admits the deployment quick fence, but selects a separate filesystem artifact store at `<repo>/.polisyos/cas` and a state root under `<repo>/.polisyos/runtime/confidence_ledger`. The production port is constructed with the existing `PromotionRuntime`, whose `store` is the runtime-supplied artifact owner, yet the writer opener does not use that store. Root/deployment identity validation therefore does not establish that production N9 ledger CAS writes use the tenant-bound or guarded runtime store. The missing bridge is specifically from the `PromotionRuntime` store owner to the production ledger session; it does not establish a tenant leak.
- **Distinct replay-reader seam (`bridge_missing`, verification `not_established`):** `_open_projected_confidence_ledger_session()` independently calls the same repo-root opener when `validate_canonical_promotion_receipt()` recomputes a receipt without an injected session. The public decision-front, EvalSafety, and export consumers reach this validation route. A writer-only store repair would not prove that these readers resolve the emitted ledger refs through the authorized runtime store or scope. Keep the replay reader as a separate review and test seam; do not claim producer-to-consumer custody until its served resolution is established.
- **Separate journal-scope premise / owner decision:** `from_repo()` places `.head.json`, `.lock`, `.append.wal`, and `.scope.json` state below the canonical repo's confidence-ledger state root; filenames derive from `ConfidenceRiskBudgetScope.scope_id`. The N9 scope constructor uses `owner_scope_key="design-problem:<design_problem_id>"`; the scope ID includes the owner ref, purpose, owner key, and epoch, but no tenant or cell. Thus matching problem-scope inputs produce a tenant-neutral journal key in this code. Whether that is an intended global, non-resettable risk budget per design problem or whether the journal must be partitioned by tenant/cell is **not established**. This is not evidence of cross-tenant access or a defect by itself. A global budget preserves one shared risk bound but pools state across tenant contexts; tenant/cell partitioning isolates journals but requires a versioned scope identity and authorized migration/replay treatment for existing heads and WALs. The confidence-ledger policy owner/principal must decide this contract before journal scope is changed.
- **P37 / P38:** the loaded deployment root/quick fence is checked, but the selected production writer's `ArtifactStore` is not an input to `from_repo()`; runtime-store use is therefore `not_established` at that gate. Tenant neutrality of journal authority is also `not_established`, pending the owner ruling. P38 divergence: the implementation's root/identity predicate can pass while a `PromotionRuntime` carries a different guarded store and `from_repo()` writes to its checkout-root CAS. Separately, two tenant contexts with the same design-problem scope fields compute the same scope ID and journal filename; the code does not decide whether that sharing is policy.
- **Smallest closure signals / falsifiers:** (1) For the writer slice, run the production N9 writer with a guarded tenant store supplied through `PromotionRuntime`; resolve its emitted scope-anchor, root, head-event, and receipt refs by bytes through that exact store, with a same-tenant positive and a foreign-tenant refusal. Keep receipt/scope markers fixed and restore the current `from_repo()` root-store route: the exact-store readback must turn red. (2) For the journal premise, record the owner decision and exercise two tenant/cell contexts with the same design-problem ID against the chosen contract: a declared global budget must demonstrate the intended shared head; a tenant/cell budget must demonstrate distinct versioned scope IDs and journals plus authorized migration/replay. Reopen the decision if the observed scope behavior differs from the chosen rule. (3) For replay, drive a public receipt consumer using only the designated runtime owner/store and reopen all emitted refs; with the same receipt markers, routing the reader back through the local `from_repo()` CAS must fail the exact-owner resolution witness. Until all three distinctions are met, do not claim full N9 ledger custody or tenant-scoped risk semantics.
- **Owner binding required before the CAS repair:** `PromotionRuntime.store` identifies the runtime artifact store, but constructing a `PromotionRuntime` does not itself appoint a Confidence Ledger authority owner. The normal guarded proxy can forward `.root` and permits unclaimed writes without an active scope, so replacing the root-path check with store injection or object identity alone would still leave the tenant-custody predicate `not_established`. A trusted serving composition must carry an owner-issued binding to a store view that requires authenticated active scope, and the same binding must reach the production writer and projected receipt readers. Preserve loaded-deployment identity, registry, risk-scope, WAL replay, and immutable-session checks; do not add caller-selectable storage to the public `from_repo()` authority opener. Until the binding and strict store view exist, the CAS repair is a design-ready bounded residual, not an authority-session fix. Do not alter journal scope until the owner decides global versus tenant/cell semantics. This premise does not block unrelated candidate-band N4/N5 work.
- **Evidence:** read-only census `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r13-store-path-census-20260929/R13_STORE_PATH_CENSUS.md@sha256:4726da338088ef2e675c1ba5ddbc3a28820ce53df7b783ed54553540382fd28f`. Current tracked anchors at `4970b3b5c`: `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/confidence_ledger.py@git-blob:0cfa16945529172584b88af2d5db0e53d4e226ab`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/promotion_sequence.py@git-blob:7f99c8239293e5e4b3710baa2baf9c89304bd7d1`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/open_world_risk.py@git-blob:ba17f9bc0ea55873af99f97db2bdbe9b642e0a3d`; replay consumers `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/evaluation_safety.py@git-blob:d390f002c09cffa24f87ddd403bd28c4e5cee1d8` and `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/public_export.py@git-blob:4d9c7f02381d12fbe0e37b61e49662095cf5037d`; pattern anchors P31/P37/P38 `policy-engine/docs/reference/policy-design-case-failure-patterns.md@git-blob:7f4cc9ab9541a44c26e4648a2c08326e53743492`. A separate extracted-wheel owner test passed on candidate fd9375a; it is not an exact-686 served-path result.

Source-independent history-corpus replay and unrelated R13/candidate-band work may proceed. Do not claim R2 byte-exact class closure, N9/S8 authority, or that absent currentness blocks ordinary candidates. No production issuer, trust restamp, or epoch reissue is needed for this verifier work.

### OP-R13-SOURCE-UPDATE — selected WDI observation period versus publisher update/as-of time (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / typed blocker:** supplemental to B12/R13 and the selected-overlay path; LA-046 remains a separate compiler/profile finding. This is one `data_record` / source-contract premise, not a new finding row, ledger status, or denominator change. It is distinct from OP-R1-TIME, which concerns the meaning and admissibility of the five DataState input time coordinates.
- **Predicate affected:** whether an admitted WDI observation may be described as the source's current or as-of vintage at a claimed time, or whether source freshness/currentness may affect a later panel selection or authority decision.
- **What the current owner path establishes:** at `96a333c43`, `acquisition_executor._raw_worldbank_row_in_scope` filters `row["date"]` against the requested year range. That is an observation-period coordinate. The passport's `source_watermark` is the SHA-256 of the raw response body; for live acquisition, `dataset_version` is the normalized-result content digest, which the live evidence validator binds to the exact raw-body digest. Data Forge verifies that body digest again during overlay admission. `CanonicalAcquisitionObservation` carries `year`, `survey_year`, and `wave`, plus those content identities; `LiveSourceExecutionEvidence` carries request/raw-response refs and content hashes but no publisher update/as-of timestamp or version semantics. These checks establish which response bytes and observation period were selected; they do not establish when the publisher considers those bytes current or which vintage they represent.
- **Missing premise and owner:** the smallest missing item is a source-issued record/contract that defines the publisher's update, publication, as-of, or vintage semantics and binds that assertion to the selected response bytes and semantic epoch. Data Forge's acquisition/overlay owner can type and verify that binding; the source publisher or its authorized custodian must supply the meaning. The relevant source-side issuer/record is not established by the cited path. A digest, local retrieval time, normalized-content version, or observation year cannot substitute for it.
- **Safe engineering now / authority boundary:** candidate N8 may continue to derive panel shape from admitted observations while carrying `source_update_time_not_established` as a typed limitation. This label is the proposed downstream limitation, not a claim that the integrated runtime currently emits it. Such work makes no source-currentness, freshness, S8, publication, or authority claim; it does not block other candidate computation. Keep OP-R1-TIME's distinct five-input DataState question separate.
- **Closure signal / falsifier:** obtain publisher-defined update/as-of/version semantics and bind the record to the selected raw-response digest, passport, and epoch; re-resolve that binding on the active read before making a currentness claim. Hold observation-year, digest, passport/epoch, and status markers fixed while removing, staling, or substituting the source-issued update/vintage assertion. If the consumer still reports the selected response current, the currentness gate is falsified and this premise reopens. If the source supplies no such semantics, preserve the typed limitation.
- **Foundry boundary:** a versioned detached Foundry receipt/context is needed only if source-currentness is later persisted and changes selection or authority. That future replay requirement is not a gate for bounded candidate N8 panel-shape work.
- **Evidence / scope:** current source blobs: `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/acquisition_executor.py@git-blob:399fc8d459ebb71169ded1da375e59c9dc24ecea`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/data_forge/domains/catalog/knowledge/overlay.py@git-blob:f59a731f7937f45ff5c56116e94903c1fe3bf358`; `policy-engine/src/polisyos/data_forge/domains/catalog/knowledge/acquisition_authority.py@git-blob:fdbbc9f0ab4aaf9c7ffd388f5512e8307aa76260`. The reviewed 04fe candidate projection is not integrated and only carries a not-established source-time limitation: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r13-exact-projection-candidate-20260928/R13_ACTIVE_OBSERVATION_PROJECTION_CANDIDATE.md@sha256:8222a875ecbe7405ba13df238261d6302c54f93395a8c63a9777061156bdefd6`; its design review distinguishes update-vintage/currentness from observation coordinates: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r13-design-review-20260929/R13_SLICE_A_REVIEW.md@sha256:bfa9b09d9d1cd0f47bf27989ea68c30f032a0f210dcf164d0e0e38ba8c4cc182` and `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r13-slice-a-rereview-20260929.md@sha256:49a9e748e824df97860020585116a687ca4ebb49541c2451c0cb22ead8d70a24`. P37: byte identity is recomputed; publisher-currentness is `not_established`. P38: treating an observation year or content digest as publisher update time would measure a proxy at the boundary this premise guards.

### OP-R13-COUNTRY-CODE-SCHEME — qualified WDI country identity versus untyped overlay strings (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / status:** subordinate to B12/R13 and separate from OP-R13-SOURCE-UPDATE. This records a missing general source-code-system contract, not a new B/LA finding or a change to the 282-row ledger. Exact WDI engineering remains available.

String shape alone cannot authorize ISO2/ISO3 equivalence for a different producer.

Preserve the original `UKR` row, passport, epoch, and hash. A non-WDI row without a bound code-system fact must remain a typed unknown/refusal. Do not infer country scope from a short token or add a global alias table.

N8 must refuse it or report an explicit unknown. A generic ISO mapping that admits the row is a failed gate. Close the broader premise only when admission records the typed code system and N8 verifies the binding on active read.

### OP-R13-CANONICAL-VARIABLE-REF — shared vocabulary gap and exact WDI binding (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / status:** nonblocking premise for the B12/R13 WDI route; LA-046 remains a separate compiler/profile finding. No new finding row, ledger status, or denominator change. Keep this separate from OP-R13-SOURCE-UPDATE and OP-R1-TIME.
- **Kind / scope:** cross-owner vocabulary contract and owner premise, `not_established` within the inspected path only. In the inspected WDI→world-growth→N8 interfaces, a shared typed `CanonicalVariableRef` contract and semantic-owner appointment are not established; this investigation does not determine whether another shared helper exists elsewhere in the repository. This bounded finding does not classify the concrete exact-ID WDI route or its Data Forge canonical-row owner as absent.
- **Claim affected:** whether a WDI observation admitted under a canonical variable identity can be selected by N8 under the *same* `DesignProblem` outcome identity, and whether the inspected path has one shared typed contract for such identities. Syntax or a metric label alone cannot establish row identity, admission, or authority.
- **Concrete engineerable route:** the inspected production route carries the exact `variable_id` from the verified planner requirement and selects the live provision whose `target_variable` equals that ID; it does not translate `metric_id`. N8 takes `DesignProblem.outcome_of_interest.target_variable` before candidate slots and queries `canonical_var` by that exact value. The current `OutcomeOfInterest.target_variable` bare-token regex rejects a qualified canonical value such as `government.balance`. Separately, the served fixture builds a problem whose outcome is `firm_survival` while its N4 fixture proposes `government.balance`; loosening the regex alone would still cause N8 to query `firm_survival`. The intended WDI outcome must be set to `government.balance` before computing the DesignProblem ref and compiled-artifact hash.
- **Owner / smallest missing capability:** reuse Data Forge catalog/alignment and overlay admission for canonical row identity, the existing IR qualified-slot grammar, and the DesignProblem/versioned schema owner. Within the inspected interfaces, the unresolved capability is a shared typed variable-reference contract and semantic-owner agreement; this finding does not assert that no such helper exists elsewhere. Do not introduce a parallel alias table. The cross-owner appointment for this universal contract is not established by the investigation.
- **Safe engineering now:** implement the exact WDI case through a versioned current DesignProblem contract (the memo recommends v3 for qualified `target_variable`), make runtime/provider schema agree, and preserve v1/v2 historical grammar, serialization, hashes, and replay. Do not globally loosen the independently exported `OutcomeOfInterest` DTO without a version boundary. Build the served positive with `government.balance` in the original problem before refs/hashes are formed; prove the same admitted `canonical_var` reaches the existing N8 L1 query. Keep an unbound dotted ID as a typed missing-data result, and retain the separate source-update limitation. No `metric_id` mapping, slug conversion, alias registry, or authority inference is needed. This concrete engineering slice does not need the broader cross-owner contract to be resolved first.
- **Closure signal / falsifier:** close the shared-contract premise only when a complete source-of-truth census shows all live producer/consumer paths use one existing typed variable-reference owner and grammar, or an owner-approved shared contract is wired through them. For the concrete route, the served positive must resolve the exact selected `government.balance` row through the existing bridge and N8 consumer. Preserve request, planner, passport, epoch, and row markers while changing or misbinding the problem outcome or selected canonical row; N8 must not return a value from a different variable. A schema-only green with the fixture still asking for `firm_survival`, or any consumer that treats `metric_id`/alias spelling as the canonical row identity, falsifies the route claim. If the row is missing after syntactic validation, diagnose it as a separate overlay/admission defect rather than masking it with an alias.
- **Evidence / limits:** read-only owner investigation pinned its executable-path diagnosis to `c1aa77ea6d44a24ce939de0ba615e71deb23faa8`; memo `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r13-variable-binding-20260929/WDI_CANONICAL_VARIABLE_BINDING.md@sha256:3e40dc06a64e880eedcd22fe047f63ea3fb2ea240f40fc1f9159830559a39c6d`. It ran no tests, did not query `production_data`, and did not census every repository mapping helper. No behavioral pass or currentness/S8 claim is recorded here. Relevant patterns: P27/P31 (reuse the row/grammar owners), P29/P32 (verify identity through real resolution), P37/P38 (reconcile the predicate and name the regex/metric proxy divergence), P40 (widen the schema class, not individual aliases).


### OP-TCS01-LEGACY-JOB-OWNER — historical NULL-owner rows and authorized migration (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / type:** TCS-01 premise only; no new B/LA row, ledger status, or denominator change. This is a `data_record` gap plus an `owner_decision` on whether and how any historical row may be migrated. An additive owner-column migration leaves pre-existing rows `(NULL, NULL)`; this note does not assert a current database row count or claim that every old row has recoverable provenance.
- **Claim affected / owner:** a TCS-01 served reader may project or consume a historical job after row scoping is added. The ControlPlaneStore owner can preserve unknown rows; an authorized principal must approve any provenance-preserving transition. In the absence of independent row-level evidence, ownership is `not_established`: do not infer it from the current caller, submitter, payload, run ID, CAS ref, or status markers. Scope is limited to TCS-01 served readers; this note makes no claim about whether separately classified internal completed-job readers can resolve legacy rows under their existing predicates.
- **Closure / falsifier:** inventory the affected legacy rows and assign each an explicit disposition: keep it unbound and inaccessible, or migrate it only with independently reconciled tenant-and-cell provenance and an authorized auditable transition. Keep row/payload/run markers while removing or substituting the owner evidence; a tenant-scoped reader must continue to hide an unbound or ambiguous row. Any reader that exposes it from caller or payload markers falsifies the boundary.
- **Safe engineering now:** persist a verified tenant plus registry-routed cell as the immutable row owner for newly served jobs and filter before projection. Keep old unknown rows hidden. This structural repair does not wait for a historical migration decision.

### OP-TCS01-CELL-REASSIGNMENT — historical job visibility after registry reassignment (2026-09-29)

- **Relation / type:** separate `owner_decision` under TCS-01; no finding-status change. The inspected `CellRegistry.register_tenant` replaces a tenant's current cell assignment. Under exact `(tenant_id, cell_id)` matching, a job recorded in the former cell is not returned under the newly routed cell. The desired historical visibility policy is not established.
- **Claim affected / owner:** whether a tenant retains access to old job status after its registry cell changes. The principal responsible for registry/reassignment policy must decide this; ControlPlaneStore owns row scoping and must not silently weaken it.
- **Closure / falsifier:** record whether reassignment leaves prior jobs inaccessible or permits access through a separately authorized reissue/rehome transition with auditable provenance. Reassign a tenant while preserving the old row and its owner markers. A route that returns the old row solely because tenant IDs match falsifies exact cell scoping; any promised continuity without an authorized transition falsifies the decision.
- **Safe engineering now:** keep each row's verified tenant/cell pair immutable and use the current registry-routed pair for served reads. A same-tenant old-cell miss remains hidden until an explicit policy and transition exist; the unresolved continuity choice does not block safe writes or exact-scope reads.

A served request persists/resolves comparator and factual-evidence refs independently, both bound to the same WMR/model, horizon and scope. The no-op yields zero; changing only the comparator leaves factual evidence unchanged. Alter/remove the binding while retaining markers and require refusal or a bounded limitation.

#### OP-SIM03-REPLICATION — count, Monte Carlo error and model uncertainty contract (B21)

- **Kind / IDs:** owner_decision; B21. Supplemental premise only; no finding status/count change.
- **Question / missing decision:** what versioned, method-specific result contract maps requested replications to actual independent/paired draws, seeds, per-outcome/per-step Monte Carlo error, and the distinct world-model uncertainty? It must not impose one replication type on every method. A single stochastic draw must remain unestimated, not zero-error.
- **Claim affected:** whether a served simulation result's precision and work performed are honestly described.
- **Owner state:** N5 replication planner/executor and Foundry paired-estimation roles are named; no individual decision/issuer appointment is recorded.
- **Safe engineering now:** keep requested-count/distinct-seed controls and the one-draw unestimated state. Candidate calculations may continue; do not report a sampling error or conflate it with world-model uncertainty when the selected method does not provide one.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** the owner adopts a typed, versioned replication/error result contract. ### OP-R14-IR-PUBLIC-ENTRYPOINT — canonical slot grammar import boundary

The kernel README presents kernel as a public/stable facade, while architecture/public_surface/contract.toml lists only polisyos.ir, polisyos.ir.analytics, and polisyos.ir.api. Evidence at e17d550bb: api.py@git-blob:aa3ec766d950a830292b8448015f2bb390080a4b; kernel/__init__.py@git-blob:defe43d23655f9e4e095fd2aa62a6031b942429a; kernel/base.py@git-blob:cbb0c4b122ecf0ac9fbebbcab84dd2348c6265c8; runtime/quality/design_problem.py@git-blob:1fe470467f7c008c1e3c0eb4a6b8b98b4bf585e2; contract.toml@git-blob:d568a925f1cf055e517f5a5e0d691d4c6390a0a5; kernel/README.md@git-blob:63de7dc5623aa08ac9b1dd1146f21f35c1e1c5f1.

- **Owner:** team-polisyos and the public-surface contract owner; no decision or appointment is inferred.
- **Options and costs:**
  1. **A — declare polisyos.ir.kernel supported.** Aligns the contract with the documented facade. Cost: certifies the broader kernel export surface, with owner review, generated inventory/reference/release updates, and ongoing compatibility stewardship.
  2. **B — expose one narrow canonical slot-ID API from already-supported polisyos.ir.** Keeps the supported entrypoint narrower. Cost: adds a kernel-owned symbol or validator to the root IR facade, requiring owner API/export tests and generated-surface refresh.
  3. **C — keep this one edge open pending the owner decision.** Avoids surface churn. Cost: the deep-import finding remains open and the current import path has no public-entrypoint closure evidence.
- **Safe engineering now:** the separate three-import R14 Core Security slice is independent of this IR premise and may proceed under its exact lease. This statement does not mark that candidate reviewed, integrated, or behaviorally verified. The current R14 lease explicitly releases design_problem.py; any later IR source change requires an explicit sequenced lease. The active R13 qualified-outcome lease also names design_problem.py and its tests.
- **Minimum closure / falsifier:** the IR owner records the exact public-entrypoint choice. For A, the supported-entrypoint contract and owner-generated public inventory/reference agree with the actual lazy facade. For B, the selected root API is an actual importable export of the canonical object or a canonical validation operation, with owner tests. The runtime consumer must preserve accepted and rejected slot identifiers. If the claimed public attribute is absent, the route is falsified; removing the export while retaining markers must make the focused public-surface/architecture check or semantic test fail. No such test was run for this draft.
- **Remainder:** this choice does not settle later slot-grammar evolution, schema-versioning, or historical replay rules; each remains governed by its own owner contract.
- **Revisit trigger / falsifier:** after an A/B owner choice, reopen if the owner-maintained contract/inventory still omits that selected entrypoint, or import through it no longer resolves to canonical `SLOT_ID_PATTERN`; either falsifies the selected route’s support/identity premise. If C is selected, reopen if a later owner decision declares A/B supported (falsifying “unresolved”) or if Runtime Quality no longer consumes this symbol (falsifying the affected-claim premise).
- **Where it binds:** only the import/public-surface boundary between runtime.quality.design_problem and the canonical IR slot grammar.

### OP-R9-PHYSICAL-CLAIM-LINEARIZATION — ambient reads pass before owner commit (reproduced, 2026-09-30)

- **Kind / relation:** reproduced escape in the existing R9 owner-first custody class, same P40 class one level deeper. This is not a new B/LA finding. R9 remains partial; no ledger or register-row change is proposed here.
- **Property and divergence (P37/P38):** while a tenant's default sidecar is published but its owner row is pending, an unscoped ambient reader must not consume the tenant's manifest or bytes. The implementation admits an unscoped read when `has_any_tenant_claim` is false; it does not coordinate that predicate with an in-flight writer. The deterministic probe pauses tenant A after the default `write_once` succeeds and before owner recording, confirms no claim exists, then observes ambient B succeed at `get_manifest`, `has=True`, `get_paths`, and `get_bytes`. Once A commits the row, B's same APIs are denied (`has=False`; the others raise `ArtifactOwnershipError`). The unclaimed ambient-candidate control still passes `put_bytes`, `has`, and `get_bytes`. P37: absence of an owner row is not evidence that no sidecar is pending. P38: the no-claim predicate diverges while the physical default manifest exists and is readable.
- **Smallest capability / owner:** close the publication/admission gap with one cross-instance, per-artifact transaction boundary observed by the writer and every read consumer. An owner-index pending/committed protocol may support crash recovery, but alone cannot prevent a reader from passing its check before the pending row is written. Reads must block or fail closed during pending publication, then re-evaluate after claim commit. The existing Core `FileSystemCAS` and `ArtifactOwnershipIndex` owners own this mechanism; the R9 principal/architect owns the custody rule. Individual appointment is not established. The instance-local artifact lock and the owner-index update lock do not span the whole publication/read path.
- **Separate `get_paths` lifetime seam:** the probe obtains raw `Path` objects before A's claim and verifies their files exist. A later `get_paths` call is denied, but the raw paths already returned are not revocable and can outlive the check. This is a distinct capability-lifetime boundary requiring a guarded read/open API or a scoped handle; a retrieval-time ownership check alone cannot close it. The current JUnit does not exercise direct use of a retained path after claim commit.
- **Status / next falsifier:** the preclaim ambient read is reproduced; B150 remains bounded to its direct typed-view claim and B153 remains the separate lock-waiter-after-exception residual. After the shared admission mechanism is implemented, repeat the same pause and require all four ambient APIs to block or refuse before A's claim, with the unclaimed-candidate positive control still passing; after commit, B stays denied. For the separate path seam, retain B's paths before commit, commit A's claim, then try direct reads through those paths; the guarded contract must prevent post-claim access or stop returning raw paths to ownership-enforced consumers. Remove only the admission mechanism while retaining sidecar/index markers and the closure probe must turn red. No follow-up probe was run here.

### OP-R10-SIGNED-STORE-CAPABILITY — optional verifier is absent from the generic store contract (2026-09-30)

- **Status / relation:** R10 remains `partial`; this records an implementation-contract limit, not a new B/LA finding or a promise about arbitrary backends. The complete tracked-source census found one production raw-string dynamic call in `PromotionSafetyOwner._source_attempt`: it discovers `verify_signature` with `getattr` and calls it with `ref: str`. The concrete `FileSystemCAS` boundary normalizes that string. `GuardedDependencyProxy` forwards call arguments unchanged. Human-decision callers route through `HumanDecisionAuthoritySink`, which converts its string ref to `ArtifactID` before delegating. No production caller in the 2,696-file Python census passes an `ArtifactRef` directly. Census: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/r10-public-signature-caller-census-20260930.md@sha256:fb6fca3948d035f1440853c1ae6032c237bc4c1a18768bb570f71136f26fa1b2`.
- **Property and divergence (P37/P38):** each backend admitted to promotion source custody must either accept/normalize canonical string IDs at its signature-verification boundary or expose a typed adapter that does so. `core.artifacts.ArtifactStore` does not declare `verify_signature`; the promotion owner tests only whether an optional method exists and is callable. P37: string normalization is `recomputed` by `FileSystemCAS`, but is `not_established` across implementations of the generic `ArtifactStore` protocol. P38: method presence/callability is the predicate the dynamic lookup measures; it does not prove that a selected backend accepts string IDs, verifies the same snapshot, or returns the expected typed verdict.
- **Smallest owner change:** keep CAS ownership in the existing store. Either define and use a narrow signed-store capability protocol with the accepted ID forms and typed result, or bind promotion composition to a concrete signed-store adapter that normalizes `str` to `ArtifactID`. Do not infer a generic backend guarantee from `FileSystemCAS` or its transparent proxy.
- **Probe, control, removal:** through each backend actually admitted by the production promotion composition, pass a valid string ID for a signed candidate and require `VALID` plus the configured principal binding; pass a malformed string and require a typed refusal before storage reads. Keep the existing `FileSystemCAS` control and candidate-only outcome (`promotion_authority_status=not_established`). Remove the normalization adapter while retaining method/marker presence; the valid-string witness must turn red. Until the backend set is named and exercised, unsupported backends remain `not_established` rather than being reported compatible.
- **Evidence:** `ArtifactStore` protocol `policy-engine/src/polisyos/core/artifacts/protocol.py@git-blob:f8c5cf431e874252de4215ae362bc0d75ff516cc`; concrete normalization `core/artifacts/store.py@git-blob:b8dc1fe4c971b0bda5e40ae25db1c7deaf41c1b9`; raw dynamic promotion caller `runtime/quality/promotion_safety.py@git-blob:a6c2fa1e2a7e3dc63d501a448f4c58ed1d8b253a`; transparent guard `runtime/http/resilience.py@git-blob:3e3fce3e300fa84d3f63f73643a94dfb3b1d4d3b`; human-decision normalizer `runtime/http/services/control/run_lifecycle.py@git-blob:7bed4d395d866f7aaa3df7930b53ca42ba2b88b0`. Existing promotion string-path test is `test_promotion_safety.py::test_signed_source_custody_never_establishes_promotion_policy`; its definition was inspected, not rerun in this addendum.


#### 2026-09-30 signed-store implementation and bounded N9 witness at `fe246c65b`

The earlier dynamic raw-string caller finding above is a pre-R10 census result. Commit `fe246c65bfa3f06fe1987ae78ae0147e3e1ebe67` replaces that N9 verifier call with a typed `ArtifactID` call through the separate optional `SignatureVerifyingArtifactStore` capability. The `ArtifactStore` contract remains backend-neutral; the capability is selected for filesystem and carried over the exact guarded store handle into the existing `PromotionSafetyOwner`. Typed verification outcomes are persisted by request schema v2, with the prior v1 projection retained. This closes the measured dynamic raw-string call at the N9 owner, not the generic all-backend contract.

The full tracked Python census at that commit covered 2,696 `policy-engine/src/**/*.py` files and 445 `policy-engine/tools/**/*.py` files, with 28 definition/call matches (9 definitions / 19 calls). It also leaves string-shaped direct calls outside this patch at `value_choice_provenance.py:821` and `obligation_coverage.py:532,589`; their behavior across other verifier backends is not established. Census script and bounded receipts, source/test identities, and P37/P38 details are in the R10 addendum in `BASELINES.md`.

The focused same-tenant/cell N9 repository path passes, and foreign tenant/cell request and source reads are refused by the guarded repository/owner. The separate `ControlWorker` route does not establish tenant custody: the worker reads tenant-claimed payload before `_job_tenant_scope(payload)`, and the witness does not install `tenant_scope` around dispatch. The configured filesystem selector is the tested predicate; the deployed backend is `not_established`. Non-filesystem verifier producers remain `producer_missing`, and the served worker tenant-custody semantic witness remains `semantic_test_missing`. Promotion authority remains `not_established`.

The exact pre-R10 bridge file has 11 red outcomes at `0ea8137ae01605a6749bfc4a8cc7b42f8db69ec9`; the post-R10 whole-file receipt preserves all 13 common outcomes and adds one passing custody selector. This proves those reds only at that exact baseline, not ownership by R1 or R14. The full four-base P41 replay remains `UNRUN`. R10 stays `partial`; this addendum changes no ledger, register, crosswalk, or plan status.

A changed-reference child index is admissible only after its BM25 ordered top-80, atom, score, certificate, and decision outputs match a cold rebuild. The two live in-memory indexes may violate the required RAM headroom; peak RSS/swap is unmeasured.
- **Predicate, residual, next falsifier:** P37 labels the full-reference content and changed-child equivalence `not_established`; a cache keyed only by declared reference hash is a P38 proxy. Measure wall time and peak RSS/swap on the real read-only `production_data` symlink, retaining 20–30% RAM headroom, and compare a child path against a cold rebuild across ordered top-80 and downstream artifacts. Then run the served selector under the unchanged timeout plus fake-target, L2-vintage, tenant/context, and authority controls. Remove only the relation-state lookup/update while retaining cache/schema markers: the served timing/property test must turn red, and the unchanged-reference control must still pass. Until then, R1 served N4→N5, real-data grounding, S8, N9, promotion and publication remain unverified. The timeout's owning contract and four-base origin are also `not_established`.

#### DuckDB FTS child-update limit (source-backed supplement; 2026-09-30)

- **Supported owner operation:** `policy-engine/uv.lock@git-blob:85f1fb2150a4da454fee02108565381d44f56730` pins DuckDB 1.4.3. The [official DuckDB 1.4 LTS FTS documentation](https://duckdb.org/docs/lts/core_extensions/full_text_search) warns that an FTS index does not update when its input table changes and names index recreation as the refresh. The repository wrapper `policy-engine/src/polisyos/runtime/quality/grounding_relation.py@git-blob:24eb77299a24eb29a5104f29e20ff02fcb5ef9ba` likewise materializes all reference edges and calls `PRAGMA create_fts_index`; it has no child-delta operation. This is a NO-GO for claiming a supported exact incremental update today, not a proof that a bespoke exact algorithm can never be implemented.
- **Exact-path consequence:** the current known exact operation for CG5 is a cold full FTS build over the updated `CredalReference`. Reusing the base index is valid only for the byte-identical base; a child reference changes the indexed corpus and may change BM25 statistics and top-80 membership. Therefore base-only caching does not close the measured CG5 path and must not be reported as a child-index hit. The previously recorded trace did not finish the child build, so full child-build duration and peak RSS/swap remain `not_established`.
- **Safe next measurement and falsifier:** after available disk is at least 8 GiB, first obtain one cold base-only RSS/swap profile with DuckDB `threads` explicitly set to 1 or 2 and a single resource-bearing process. Attempt the served base-plus-child run only if measured headroom can preserve the 20–30% physical-RAM reserve for both indexes; otherwise keep the full-child measurement `UNRUN`. Record separate base/child build durations, process peak RSS, host memory pressure, and swap delta, with the served timeout unchanged. A future owner-supported child update must equal an independent cold child rebuild on ordered `(edge_key, BM25 score)` top-80, retrieved atom IDs/reasons/scores, CG1 certificate bytes/hash, and downstream CG2/CG3 records over the controlled served inputs and adversarial one-edge insert/replace cases. If the cold path misses the existing budget or reserve, retain the residual; do not increase the timeout or substitute the base index.

#### Base-only profile and attempt-5 comparability correction (2026-09-30)

- **Base-only measurement:** A one-process served-selector profile at scratch HEAD `ebc00a015dc6f2fb8ad0ae948403274405bfe7e9` built the 604,530-edge base full-reference FTS index in 27.201 seconds. Peak process `ru_maxrss` was 5,575,737,344 bytes; swap grew by 415.81 MiB and triggered the approved resource guard before the changed-reference child index build. The run is a resource-bounded stop, not a product failure. Child build duration/RSS, complete served selector, removal probe, and preserving control are `UNRUN`; four-base performance provenance remains `not_established`.
- **Attempt-5 comparability correction:** Attempt 5 used the same scratch worktree and HEAD, but retained no source or working-test blob pins. Exact source/test comparability is therefore `not_established`; matching HEAD alone does not pin the dirty fixture. Its 121.728-second selector span and approximately 81-second first-prewarm span are different measurement boundaries from this profile's 27.201-second inner FTS build and must not be combined or compared. The latter is not child-build timing.
- **Bounded conclusion and evidence:** The measured base build does not establish the changed-reference child cost; the cold full-index rebuild remains the exact production path. Next, measure that cold rebuild and the served selector inside the stated safe resource envelope, with the timeout and BM25 semantics unchanged. If the cold path completes within budget and preserves the RAM/disk reserve, retain it. Only if it exceeds the existing budget or reserve should an owner-supported exact incremental update be considered, and only with cold-rebuild equivalence over ordered top-80 retrieval and downstream outputs; otherwise retain the residual. The current child run, removal probe, and preserving control remain `UNRUN`. Corrected outcome memo `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r1-cg5-base-only-20260930/R1_CG5_PERFORMANCE_OUTCOME.md@sha256:75d198417445c70634c6a820fdbf157e0a74c5120be7aee14e5dcbed7c3a9d7d`; measurement receipt `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r1-cg5-base-only-20260930/r1-cg5-performance-receipt.json@sha256:524fa63f6bad45638f4d1f51ea692d59bb1a19f82ca26f7138b5d1262a822bd5`.
