Other blocked causes share the same run-status branch. No N9 bypass is identified by this source inspection.
- **Capability A — production Cycle Board enumeration (`absent/unallocated`; `artifact_missing` + `bridge_missing`):** the current typed board contract names `production_recursive_cycle_run_enumeration`, limits known scope to the N10 capstone plus legacy fixture cohorts, marks future production scope `not_established`, and fixes `exhaustive=false`. The upstream depth-N projector iterates only the `domain_runs` mapping supplied by its owner; this is not a complete N6 production denominator. The existing owner route is GY-N12. GY-GAP5 permits additive content-bound start/resolution events in the existing CAS and diagnostic event log; recorder failure must not change the cycle terminal. **Smallest capability:** N12's complete event/run membership artifact and typed bridge into the Board. **Falsifier:** remove or substitute one decisive event while keeping the row, terminal and reason markers; replay must lose the membership/cause or return a typed gap, never remain exhaustive. A verified complete N12 ledger and an ordinary completed/blocked control must both reach the served governed Board. Until then, Board coverage is unknown outside its declared cohorts.
- **Capability B — replayable safety-cap cause (`not_established`, cause attribution only):** `max_cycles` is an input to the live N6 loop but is not persisted in `GenerationCycleRun`. The current validator accepts `voi_safety_cap_reached_without_scheduler_stop` with final `next_action="advance"` shape without recomputing that the configured cap equals the observed cycle count. P37: the cap-attainment predicate is `not_established` from the historical run. P38: the implementation tests the reason and action markers, so a shorter otherwise-valid run carrying the cap reason is the divergent case. This does **not** relax terminality: the enclosing run remains `blocked` and receives no N9 source. **Smallest capability / owner:** the existing N6 run/history owner must persist a content-bound cap/configuration basis with the run and make attainment replayable. Extending the hashed run requires a schema-version bump and historical serializer that excludes the new field for old records. **Falsifier:** a run at the recorded cap with no scheduler stop replays as cap-blocked; changing/removing its cap basis or shortening the run while preserving reason/action markers must make validation fail. Historical records must remain byte-exactly replayable and must not gain current authority.
- **Movement-surface clarification:** the served Cycle Board carries typed `MovementRecord` values, including the deeper movement's `terminal_kind` and `reentry_receipt_ref`; the dashboard adapter forwards those records. That terminal kind describes the deeper acquisition re-entry terminal, not N6 `voi_decision.next_action`. The route receipt binds `compiled_ref`; resolving that artifact yields the compiled recursive run containing the source N6 run. The Board exposes the run ID and movement record, but does not project `voi_decision.next_action` or infer N9 eligibility from the deeper movement terminal. Its movement set remains scoped to known rows and `exhaustive=false`; the lack of a denormalized action label is not a third R11 bypass.
- **Evidence limits:** current-head evidence here is a targeted source inspection, not a fresh full consumer census, a behavioral removal probe, or a four-base P41 replay. The complete tracked-source matrix at `1e4618ae3` is source-only and predates current HEAD; it found no N9 bypass but does not cover current code. Historical R11 focused JUnit exists (public-export group 2/2: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r11-integrated-20260927/R11_PUBLIC_EXPORT_FOCUSED_V4.junit.xml@sha256:3664c2035c16d8eba5a38319928602487941616e7fd56d830016b4659667c7cf`; history suite 26/26: `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r11-integrated-20260927/R11_HISTORY_FULL.junit.xml@sha256:7c574c748eca6d45de718fa53bd04ec8c26595cb1dafc3183f05278774c5f404`). ### OP-R9-PRODUCTION-APPROVAL-OWNERSHIP-V3 — served consumer binding remains unimplemented (2026-09-30)

- **Status / relation:** R9/CAS-01 remains `partial`; this is the approval-consumer and rollout half of the existing owner-first custody class, not a new B/LA finding or closure claim. Commit `8765436bbe0311e5a5a131efceb4754fed0a35cf` landed the immutable generation/pointer writer, but it publishes a pointer on every changed owner mutation without a deployment-bound activation precondition. The v3 consumer/activation gate is `UNRUN`; the v2 authority path remains reachable and unsafe if pointer format is active. No production CAS was inspected, so actual external pointer/migration state is `not_established`.
- **Production path and divergence (P37/P38):** `runtime/http/routes/runs.py::create_run_production_approval` resolves the tenant and current scorecard, basis, decision record and request; `runtime/quality/approval.py::build_resolved_production_approval_packet` still emits v2 and copies only two semantic owner digests into generic `evidence_refs`; `ProductionApprovalPacketResolver.persist_authorized_packet` hands it to `HumanDecisionService` for canonical bytes, CAS ref/hash, signature, readback and durable-event reconciliation. Later `require_currentness` re-resolves approval inputs, but neither it nor `resolve_production_decision_packet` resolves owner evidence, so the v2 reader can still report current after an ordinary mutation has published a pointer or changed a selected claim. The route also treats owner-evidence lookup failure as absent evidence. Gate predicates for raw bytes and scoped claims must be recomputed by the canonical owner; claim-to-manifest correspondence must be independently reconciled. The served v3 consumer/activation gate is `UNRUN`; this source statement is not evidence of a live deployed pointer.
- **Owner evidence: landed mechanism versus missing consumer:** Commit `8765436bb` adds immutable generation/pointer parsing and writing. Current `ArtifactOwnershipIndex.evidence()` emits the v3 envelope version, `pointer_generation_v1`, separate pointer/generation schema IDs, exact pointer and generation byte hashes, semantic payload/signature digests, the portable locator `{container_ref:<generation_sha256>, member:"signature"}`, and a pre-v3 history status; `ownership_index_generation_path` is diagnostic. The owner-local `index.lock` serializes index mutation but is not a runtime `FileSystemCAS` lease. This is summary evidence, not the required scoped approval-claim resolver: it does not return a recomputed selected scorecard/basis/decision/request projection under the store lease. The following normalized envelope is the proposed consumer contract, not a completed v3 approval capability:
- **Packet/currentness contract:** v3 carries a strict nested `artifact_ownership_binding`, never more keys in `evidence_refs: dict[str,str]`. It carries the envelope/storage/payload/pointer/generation schema IDs, semantic digests, exact pointer/generation hashes, portable locator `{container_ref:<generation_sha256>, member:"signature"}`, and owner-computed `scoped_claims_digest`; it excludes local absolute paths. The owner computes a sorted projection from the server-resolved exact scorecard, production basis, human-decision record and decision-request refs, tenant/cell, and each selected manifest profile. Issuance and currentness recompute the same projection under the runtime-supplied store lease. Preserve issuance pointer/generation refs for replay; compare the scoped projection, not the whole global generation, so an unrelated tenant write does not stale this approval. Missing, extra, ambiguous or unverifiable claims yield typed unresolved/refusal, never `None` or a digest-only fallback. Re-resolve under the same lease as the authority CAS write.
- **Historical bytes and current behavior:** The target serializer remains version-selected: v1 is its exact original field projection (the legacy helper’s `exclude_none=True` encoding stays distinct); v2 preserves its full original authority projection, including default/`None` values; v3 is exact v2 projection plus strict owner binding. A re-dump through an expanded model is not a fixture. Current production code still emits v2 and its currentness consumer ignores owner evidence; after pointer publication it can still report a v2 packet current. The required v3 behavior is that v1 stays history-only and v2 cannot be emitted after activation or produce operational currentness. A v2 digest-pair match is only a snapshot match; owner-binding `historical-valid` requires recomputing exact packet-scoped tenant/cell/selected-manifest claims from captured bytes, otherwise typed `UNRESOLVED`/`snapshot_match_only`, separate from packet signature status. Pre-v3 packets need authorized reissue to regain current authority; never infer generation hashes.
- **Repository denominator / data gap:** the complete committed-tree census at source pin `eb3383e3581b0ac7dc55781934584a1bde91dc7e` enumerated 13,413 tracked `policy-engine` paths and all 290 committed `tests/fixtures` files; zero fixture files contain raw v1/v2 approval packets or signed receipts. Dynamic model/test construction is not a historical-byte fixture. This is an empty committed fixture census, not a zero live population. The configured/persisted production CAS packet inventory, owners and retention remain `not_established` until enumerated by the canonical store owner. Do not claim historical byte-exact replay for absent records.
- **Complete ordered production write set:** (1) `core/artifacts/ownership.py` + `core/artifacts/store.py`: generation/pointer writer landed; remaining owner-owned deployment-bound activation gate and lease-bound scoped resolver; (2) `core/contracts/control.py` strict nested DTO/discriminator; (3) `runtime/quality/approval.py` v1/v2/v3 serializers and builder, v2 emission fence; (4) `runtime/http/services/human_decisions.py` manifest/producer/reader versions, pre-write revalidation, signed readback and current/history classification; (5) `runtime/http/routes/runs.py`, composition/container and deployment-security attestation, with typed owner failure and canonical loaded-code/lock identity; (6) generated API owner, OpenAPI snapshot and runtime client/dashboard through their generators; (7) owner, serializer, service, deployment-trust and served-route tests; (8) DS9 runtime/schema docs and release fragment. These are the complete planned owner families, not a claim that every importer was enumerated.
- **Ordered rollout gate (required before production activation):** The current writer has no activation switch, so first add an owner/config-owned, canonical-deployment-bound default-off gate covering every pointer publication path; do not use an independent environment toggle. Until that exists, a pointer may be published by an ordinary changed ownership mutation. Add a HumanDecisionService/resolver guard so v2 issuance refuses before CAS authority write and a v2 read cannot return operational currentness whenever pointer format is observed. Then freeze v1/v2 serializer bytes/refs and enumerate live CAS through the canonical owner; implement/review the lease-bound scoped resolver and strict packet v3 DTO/writer/readers; regenerate API surfaces through owners; attest canonical v3 writer/readers and drain all v2 writers; only then perform authorized first-pair capture and one-way pointer activation. After activation, v2 stays history-only; v3 currentness requires exact owner verification and matching scoped projection. No receipt is restamped. Ordinary candidate/runtime storage remains available under the inactive format.
- **Behavioral falsifier/control:** keep packet markers and its signature valid while changing/removing a relevant owner claim; the served currentness path must become typed stale/unresolved and issue no currentness receipt. Change only another tenant's claim while the four scoped claims stay fixed; the served control must remain current. The fixture census and live-store inventory are separate acceptance gates.
- **Evidence / status:** design/caller review `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r9-approval-v3-owner-binding-design-20260930.md@sha256:a6457ef432cf99deb810dc73c8d63252519f3c42a733ff9ddcc8791324298e32`; independent pointer-rollout review `/Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-r9-pointer-rollout-20260930/R9_POINTER_ROLLOUT_GATE_REVIEW.md@sha256:4a5ddae35f92d134328798d95bc4adb522cf8b3ab225788a3288a2c9be409e2d`; landed owner code `8765436bbe0311e5a5a131efceb4754fed0a35cf`. These are source/design records only: the v3 consumer/activation gate is `UNRUN`, with no production CAS inspection or behavioral witness. P04/P05/P07/P08/P27/P29/P31/P32/P37/P38/P40 apply; no register, plan or finding status changes.
#### Current-state correction — v2 authority containment is implemented; v3 positive path remains open (2026-09-30)

- **Provenance and supersession:** the preceding bullets preserve the pre-containment source/design review at the 8765436 owner-writer integration. They are not the current served-state description. R9 code and its focused evidence landed in `6722e2a30ea424c84b1d20dfaa64664ee045fdec`; this addendum supersedes only claims that the v2 authority path remains reachable through the shared human-decision owner. It does not erase the original proposal or imply a principal ruling. A narrower interim requires owner-atomic publication gating first.
- **Current owner mechanism:** `_write_payload` still publishes immutable pointer generations on every ownership mutation; there is no global activation gate or deployment-bound pointer toggle. The v2 containment is centralized at `HumanDecisionService`, using owner evidence from the runtime-supplied `HumanDecisionAuthoritySink`. `create_run_production_approval` → `ProductionApprovalPacketResolver.persist_authorized_packet` → `_persist_production_decision_packet` returns typed refusal before packet CAS write for every recognized owner state. `resolve_production_decision_packet`, the shared currentness consumer for `ProductionApprovalPacketResolver.require_currentness`, `AgentActionAuthorityBuilder`, and capability authority, refuses v2 operational currentness for empty-v2, legacy-v1, v2 signed-pair, and pointer-generation states. The nonempty v2 signed-pair case is source-inspected in the helper's schema-v2/absent-pointer branch, not separately behavior-tested. Owner lookup/shape failure is separately typed unresolved. The route helper's `None` projection is not used as proof of owner currentness. Candidate-band CAS writes remain available.
- **P37/P38:** owner format is read from the supplied store and checked; exact packet-scoped claim correspondence is still `not_established`. The implemented consumer consequently has no recognized v2 pass path. P38 divergence is intentional over-refusal: even an old signed-pair packet with apparently matching digest markers cannot be operationally current until a reader independently resolves its scoped claims. This is not evidence that its signature or historical bytes are invalid. The test confirms one historical signed packet's canonical bytes and custody signature remain readable while the operational reader refuses it. The committed fixture census remains zero raw approval packet/receipt fixtures; dynamic fixture construction is not exhaustive historical replay evidence, and the live CAS population remains `not_established`.
- **Required v3 positive path:** still `UNRUN` and unimplemented. The owner must produce a strict nested binding with exact pointer/generation byte identity and locator plus a recomputed projection of the server-resolved scorecard, basis, decision record, request, tenant/cell, and selected manifest profiles under the runtime-supplied store lease. Issuer and fresh reader must recompute that same scope. With packet markers retained, removing/changing a selected claim must refuse; changing only another tenant's claim must preserve currentness. A deployment-bound v2 drain/activation receipt and a verified production inventory are still required before any global rollout claim. No production CAS was inspected.
- **P40:** this is the same R9 owner-first custody class, widened to the shared approval authority seam. The central refusal covers the tested HumanDecisionService issuer/reader. If an additional production v2 issuer or reader bypasses the owner, route it through this mechanism; do not add another per-consumer gate. Full importer enumeration remains open.

A nonempty v2 signed-pair state is covered only by source inspection of the schema-v2/absent-pointer refusal branch; it was not separately behavior-tested.

### OP-EMP01-B31-STATISTICAL-INTERVAL — source interval is erased or reinterpreted (2026-09-30)

- **Class/status:** Existing held B31 under EMP-01. The 282/282 ledger and B31=held remain unchanged; this note is not a finding-level closure or register proposal.
- **Attribution:** The fresh run at source HEAD `237b0d0d0` reports both selectors RED: test_identification_set_and_statistical_uncertainty_remain_separate and test_asymmetric_interval_is_preserved_by_value_projection (JUnit /Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/e02-b31-current-20260930/B31_current_two.junit.xml@sha256:1a3a2cb4abb50065f7bf30ebb9225210a10f4bb74b1acad985bac46cab750b32). These are not pass→fail regressions: test_emp_01.py is absent at E02 execution base 78187878e and main 5fd3ebcc1; it was added test-first on E02, and its current blob matches E02 head. There is no recorded passing base for these assertions. The implementation contract and projector are unchanged across those refs; therefore do not open R14 or attribute the red to R2.
- **Observed divergence (P37/P38):** ValueOuterSet.lower/upper represents identification bounds, but has no statistical interval channel. The Foundry result helper drops a native CI for point identification; for proxy identification it synthesizes a symmetric interval from CI width, estimate magnitude and a fixed floor. Thus [1,10] around point 4 is either lost beside the point set or changed to [-0.5,8.5]. A confidence-interval marker does not establish its coverage, estimand, unit, source, or time scope.
- **Owner and bounded next step:** Extend the existing ValueOuterSet owner only after the principal/contract owner resolves interval semantics; do not add an adjacent DTO or infer authority. The helper currently has no production caller and no production ValueGateReceipt constructor was found, so a constructor-only fix cannot close B31 (implemented_but_not_orchestrated; numerical surface surface_missing). Candidate computation may continue with the missing meaning carried as a typed limitation; no promotion or publication claim follows.
- **Falsifier/control:** With point, identification bounds, status and source markers intact, remove the separately persisted statistical interval: the consumer/recomputed receipt must expose a typed limitation and must not claim complete uncertainty. Keep [1,10] asymmetric through persistence/reopen; a rescaled 100× case must scale both bounds 100×. Point-identification control keeps lower=upper=point and candidate computation available while preserving the separate interval.
- **Evidence/direction:** Audit card source/B_r19_original.md@git-blob:d800082eebfc12eaf647b0c0257c4f06e135d25a; existing owner core/contracts/value_outer_set.py@git-blob:e73454e6b50624acc69627f48172a7612c9ccd9c; projector function SHA-256 aaa9d1868791a8ee6805f860c1d527d8de3365c513f36b76c96a06dd1dfc8f3e; four-ref/source/consumer trace /Users/deniskopylov/.codex/scratch/E02R2-B31-statistical-interval-design-dee6ab3af.md@sha256:dce7d1456cd5a85db252f77448d85597aaaaa3c9fc8af384c3613bcf60d33a86. GY-N8 and Atlas DS16 favor separate typed uncertainty and producer-owned projections; P03/P07/P10/P31 apply. Historical snapshots/receipts need versioned serializers that preserve old bytes; live CAS inventory is not established.

The property is narrow and load-bearing: before N4 for any protected `sandbox_pilot`, `field_pilot`, or `deployment` leaf, the recursive owner recomputes the graph leaf denominator, requires an exact intent/context mapping, and verifies the current EvalSafety head bound to the same problem and mode. Preserve both records and the selected observation; do not label it a causal E02 regression until P41 establishes same-input closure and owner.

The corrected R5 owner work later passed a bounded 28/28 wave with a marker-retaining removal receipt. That establishes the protected gate at that later snapshot, not who introduced the earlier red. #### R14 — reviewed integration has bounded passes and one setup error; served proof remains open

The V3 lifecycle review gives bounded GO for the code candidate; the V4 test-only delta had a separate chat-only GO. The v2 core4 wave initially returned 50/53, but the independent worker review found all three acquisition-worker reds shared one invalid direct fixture: empty policy flags, a fake `{"capability":"ds15"}` manifest, and no authenticated owner admission. ## V5 B197 source, served-intake, and typed-consumer reconciliation — 2026-10-01

This addendum refines B197’s one held residual after a read-only census of frozen commit `28ea3bb56bc4e751bf1303615d79219b56d7a24c`. The census walked 2,906 tracked `src/polisyos` paths, including and AST-parsing all 2,696 production Python files (zero parse failures), plus all 272 tracked deployment/config paths in its declared scope. It found no production construction of `Calibrator`/`CalibratorInputs` and no executable caller of `put_calibration_report()`. The census ran no tests. It does not retract or re-run the separate canonical internal 58/58 whole-file witness; the receipt and JUnit include `test_calibrator_tied_report_reaches_delta_and_monte_carlo_welfare`, which proves the internal Calibrator → v2 report persist/reopen → welfare consumer path within its declared test scope. It does not prove a configured/served production producer.

- **Served intake versus producer:** `WorkflowRunRequest.calibration_report_ref` is forwarded to the control-run lifecycle and becomes a pre-existing `ArtifactRef`; the intake assigns `foundry.calibration_report` from the request field and validates ID syntax, without binding the stored manifest/payload. The default served transition is WorkspaceLoop, not the Scientist DAG; the legacy-shadow experiment branch is marked candidate-only. The served field therefore is not a B197 producer or evidence of authority-bearing consumption.
- **Typed report-key boundary:** in the reachable internal `policy_design` workflow, the same `INPUT_CALIBRATION_REPORT_REF` is read by both V2 propagation nodes and `RunPolicyBlueprintRuntimeNode`. The latter parses `FunnelCalibrationReport` (`scientist.search.calibration_report`, schema `polisyos.scientist.search.FunnelCalibrationReport@1.0`), while B197 uses `CalibrationReport` (`foundry.calibration_report`, schema `polisyos.foundry.CalibrationReport@2.0`). Keep these models distinct. This is an actual reachable typed-boundary mismatch; it is not evidence that the default served route emitted authority or that a B197 report was produced.
- **Property / implementation divergence (P38):** property — a persisted report is admitted only when its resolved manifest kind/schema and payload agree with the specific typed consumer. Current served intake labels from the request ref and validates ID syntax; the shared policy-design key permits a Foundry V2 ref to reach a Funnel parser. Divergent case — a Foundry V2 report accepted by a propagation consumer is parsed under the distinct Funnel model at the policy-design runtime boundary.
- **Smallest capability:** one configured/served B197 Calibrator producer with independently admitted source/graph/plan provenance, through the existing report owner, followed by consumer-specific typed intake that resolves/validates stored kind/schema. No second calibrator or report owner is proposed. Synthetic candidate-band engineering can proceed without institutional data or authority; institutionally authorized real targets and source authority remain `not_established`.
- **Falsifier and preserving control (proposed; absent/UNRUN):** supply persisted Foundry V2, Funnel, and unknown-kind report refs through the candidate policy-design workflow. Only the matching typed consumer may proceed; Foundry V2 must not parse as Funnel or be admitted to an incompatible consumer, Funnel must not be treated as B197 uncertainty, and unknown/mismatched manifests must be withheld with a typed limitation. Remove manifest kind/schema resolution or route-specific typed-ref admission while leaving request kind/status markers intact; the property test must turn red. A valid compatible report and a no-report ordinary candidate request remain runnable. This is a proposed acceptance signal, not a test receipt.

## V6 R1/R2/R9 N6 selected source-view replay — bounded residual (2026-10-01)

This source-pinned review identifies a persisted N6 source-custody gap at the same selected-manifest-view class already tracked across R1/R2 and R9. It does not change any finding status or ledger count (282 rows: 8 closed, 261 partial, 12 held, 1 open), close a register row, or authorize a new principal transition. The broader R2/R9 proposals remain drafts.

- **Property and divergence (P38):** every persisted N6 source handoff must reopen through the exact honest manifest view returned by the producer. `GenerationSourceRepository.persist()` receives an `ArtifactRef` from the store but returns only `str(ref.artifact_id)`; `GenerationCycleRun.source_handoff_refs` and `GenerationSourcePreservationReceipt.source_refs` persist strings, and current readers reopen through the default-view path. When identical bytes have a different default manifest profile, the selected source-owner view is lost and the reader can report `generation_source_owner_profile_mismatch` / `source_replay_failed`. The implementation checks a bare ID/default profile; the property is exact selected-view identity.
- **Reachable consumers:** same-run `_candidate_scenario_refusal` calls `repository.load`; `_promotion_source_context` calls `resolve`; `_source_preservation_receipt` calls `preservation_receipt`; `_restore_source_run` restores the ID-only parent list and recomputes its receipt; and the production EvalSafety retained-child path loads `cycle.source_handoff_refs`. All must consume one versioned source-ref representation and existing manifest lineage owner.
- **N5/N6 boundary:** N5 v3's selected `n4_source_ref` may replay its own execution/input artifact. It does not repair persisted `GenerationCycleRun` lineage. R1's ephemeral verified worker scope is also distinct from the durable N6 source parent. Neither is evidence that a new N6 run can replay the selected nondefault source view. Keep current or historical authority fail-closed if the exact view cannot be reopened.
- **Smallest capability / owner:** extend the existing N6 source-reference projection to preserve the producer's typed `ArtifactRef`/manifest-profile selector through persisted runs and the existing readers, using the existing artifact store and manifest owners. Neither this versioned N6 representation nor a generic existing lookup that recomputes a unique source-view selector exists at the reviewed pin. This is an engineering capability missing in the repository, not a blocker for external data, institution, or owner appointment. Do not add another store or source owner.
- **Proposed engineering next step (not implemented):** freeze historical N6 v1-v4 serializer projections and bytes; version the projection for new runs; carry the selected typed source ref through `GenerationCycleRun.source_handoff_refs` and `GenerationSourcePreservationReceipt.source_refs`; and update each listed reader to resolve by the same selected manifest lineage. Do not rewrite, restamp, or reissue historical runs. Preserve the reviewed historical serializers byte-exactly.
- **Proposed closure witness set — NOT IMPLEMENTED:** served selector `tests.integration.runtime_quality.test_evaluation_safety_admission::test_served_n6_replays_selected_nondefault_source_view_after_restart`; reader matrix `tests.unit.runtime.quality.test_generation_cycle::test_n6_persisted_source_refs_replay_exact_nondefault_manifest_view_through_all_readers`; and historical control `tests.unit.runtime.quality.test_generation_cycle_history::test_historical_v1_v4_source_projections_replay_byte_exactly`. The served selector must persist and reopen an N6 run through the retained-child EvalSafety consumer after restart, without a live source checkout. Exercise identical bytes under two honest manifest views with the source-owner view nondefault and confirm the exact selected profile is used. The reader matrix covers every existing N6 reader above. The removal probe `tests.unit.runtime.quality.test_generation_cycle::test_drop_selected_manifest_profile_with_markers_retained_turns_n6_replay_red` must fail when selector propagation is removed but ID/status markers stay; the preserving control `tests.unit.runtime.quality.test_generation_cycle::test_default_view_replay_and_foreign_tenant_refusal_controls` keeps valid default-view replay green and refuses foreign-tenant reads through the guarded store. These identities are proposed acceptance signals only; no test was authored or run.
- **P40 / authority boundary:** bucket this as the same selected-source-view custody class at the N6 parent/consumer boundary, not a new behavior class or a set of reader-instance defects. Widen the shared versioned reference and read path once. This note does not resolve the broader R2/R9 principal proposals or request new permission for authority, promotion, publication, reissue, or restamp.

### OP-ARCH-EXECUTION-PROFILES — cross-owner execution profile boundary and invalidator order (pending; 2026-10-01)

- **Status and relation.** This is a missing architecture/owner-appointment premise, not an adopted ruling or a new B/LA finding. E02-ARCH-01 and E02-ARCH-08 are recommendations; the prior 44-item traceability audit marks both `partially_recorded`. Its linked finding rows are traceability only and no ledger status changes follow. Denis's R1 controlled-candidate and R9 V2 production-approval refusal decisions remain binding within their stated scopes; this premise neither broadens a refusal nor reopens those choices.
- **Property and divergence (P37/P38).** If strict-local protected ordering is selected, the covered admission, publication, or current-use action must evaluate one coherent authoritative view of every invalidator named for that action. E02-ARCH-08's divergent execution is concrete: Core reads fence 7 and an active permit; Control commits cancellation/fence 8; Core commits a protected result against its stale read. Per-owner or instance-local checks do not establish that cross-owner order. The required physical transaction domain and its action roster are not yet appointed or verified.
- **Profiles and owner boundary.** E02-ARCH-01 distinguishes candidate/history computation from strict-local protected issuance/current-use and retains ordinary authorization, tenant, source-access, job-custody, DataTrust, and EvalSafety duties. Runtime authority, artifact custody, and epoch lifetime are distinct responsibilities; the cross-owner appointments and shared boundary are `not_established`. Same-host SQLite/WAL and filesystem/runtime assumptions are candidates to qualify, not evidence that a required domain already exists. Operated multi-host guarantees remain unclaimed.
- **Existing work and smallest missing capability.** Reuse existing Core artifact/ownership, Control, and N11 owners and their transaction/intake paths; do not create a parallel owner. `OP-R9-PHYSICAL-CLAIM-LINEARIZATION` covers a narrower CAS owner/read-publication race; `OP-R4-N11-LIFETIME` covers N11 exposure lifetime. Neither establishes the full ARCH-08 invalidator domain. The smallest unresolved capability is an appointed cross-owner contract plus a complete source action/invalidator census, followed by verification of one physical transaction/admission boundary for only the actions selected. Candidate/history work continues under declared owner-local limits and typed unknowns; this gap alone does not refuse it.
- **Revisit signal and falsifier.** Keep the invalidator/admission markers intact. Pause after the authoritative permit/fence read, commit cancellation or a relevant trust/policy/N11 invalidation, then resume the covered protected action. If it remains admitted on the stale revision, the selected ordering property is false. The ordinary candidate/history control must remain available and carry unknowns as typed limitations. This is a proposed acceptance signal, not a test receipt.
- **Where it binds.** Only the protected actions later named in an appointed strict-local contract. It does not block ordinary candidate computation or historical replay, authorize a protected action, or change R1/R9 rulings. No finding status, ledger row, register, or plan status changes are proposed.

## OP-R1-CONTEXT evidence addendum — raw-context purpose seam (source review, 2026-10-02)

This is a bounded source observation inside the existing R1 context/authority premise, not a new finding, owner appointment, or behavior result. The read-only review reports that `resolve_cycle_substrate_context` gives a `CandidateSimulationContextOffer` a `CandidateSimulationContextHandoff`, but its raw `CycleSubstrateContext` branch persists/replays and returns the context without that handoff. The recursive path validates its execution intent and context, yet leaf construction passes the context and optional handoff to `GenerationCycleController` without the intent; the standard container checks the exact configured admission owner only when candidate profiles are configured, while the injected control-service path can accept an arbitrary callable owner. This is a reachable composition seam, not evidence that N9 or S8 authority can actually be reached; downstream refusal remains possible and unmeasured.

Keep ordinary candidate computation available with a typed limitation. Do not treat candidate/S8 markers as proof of owner purpose, and do not blanket-refuse candidate N9. The same-class falsifier is a raw typed candidate-only context with its limitation markers intact through a `simulate_only` route; verify that N6/N9/S8 consumers cannot upgrade it into an authority-bearing result while the ordinary candidate control still computes.
